[SYSTEM]
You are an RTL (Register Transfer Level) design agent. Your task is to create a Verilog/SystemVerilog design that satisfies the given specification, is functionally correct, and has minimal cost (area_delay_product).

**Cost metric `area_delay_product`:** area × achieved critical-path delay — the classic area-delay product. Does NOT include simulation cycle count (contrast with the 'area_runtime_product' metric). Cost is measured on your design alone. The testbench and everything it owns (checking logic, the external memories, etc.) is not synthesized and contributes nothing to area, delay, power, or energy.

## Specification
Weight-stationary systolic matrix-multiply tile (a Google-TPU-style MXU),
module name `tpu`, implementing the EXACT per-cycle behavior of the logikbench
`tpu` benchmark (zeroasiccorp/logikbench, MIT).

FUNCTION: computes C = A * B tile products on an 8x8 grid of processing
elements. B (the "weight" matrix, 8-bit signed) is loaded into the array and
held; A (the "activation" matrix, 8-bit signed) streams through; result rows
C (32-bit signed) drain out the bottom.

PROTOCOL:
  1. Weight load: assert w_valid for 8 cycles; w_data carries one weight row
     per cycle, 8 signed bytes packed { col 7 ... col 0 }, BOTTOM matrix row
     first (cycle k carries B[7-k][*]; the weights shift south). After 8
     cycles PE(i,j) holds B[i][j].
  2. Compute: assert a_valid while streaming A one row per cycle (packed the
     same way). Row m of the result, C[m][*] packed { col 7 ... col 0 } as 8
     signed 32-bit words, appears on c_data with c_valid high exactly 15
     (= 2*8-1) compute cycles after its A row was accepted.
  - The array advances only while w_valid is low: asserting w_valid stalls
    any in-flight computation (and reloads weights). Zeros are injected while
    a_valid is low, so idle/drain cycles add nothing to the accumulators.
  - Multiple A tiles may stream back-to-back against the same loaded weights.

CYCLE ACCURACY: c_valid is compared against the original design EVERY cycle;
c_data is compared on every cycle where c_valid is high. The 15-cycle
compute-latency and the stall semantics are part of the contract.

Ports:
  clk, rst (async active-high reset, asserted only before the stream begins)
  w_valid, w_data[63:0]
  a_valid, a_data[63:0]
  c_valid, c_data[255:0]

A working starting point is provided: `starting_point.v` is the
original logikbench RTL (tpu_pe / tpu_array / tpu: PE mesh, per-row input
skew, per-column output de-skew, valid pipeline). It passes every cycle of the golden
trace in `vectors.dat` (directed zero/identity/extreme tiles, random tiles,
back-to-back streams, a_valid gaps, mid-drain weight reloads, random soak).
Study it, then optimize while preserving the cycle-accurate behavior. The
sibling benchmark `tpu_spire` shares the identical testbench and vectors
with a SpireHDL starting point.

## Tools
- create_file: Create a new file in your working directory
- replace_file: Replace the entire contents of an existing file
- apply_diff: Apply a unified diff to modify a file
- edit_file: Edit a file by replacing an exact string match with new content. Provide old_string (must match exactly once) and new_string.
- ls: List files in your working directory
- read_file: Read the contents of a file
- run_evaluation(filename[, target_delay]): Run evaluation on the given design file. Returns correctness (Verilator simulation) + cost (Yosys area_delay_product). The filename must be your main design file (e.g. 'design.sv'). Optionally pass target_delay (ps) to override the synthesis timing constraint.

## Strategy
1. Lay out an action plan. Try to cover a diverse set of approaches in your plan to increase the chances of finding a good solution within the step limit. Also once you find a new best solution, explore close solutions. Trade off exploration with exploitation.
2. First, create a simple, straightforward design that is functionally correct. Focus on getting 100% correctness first.
3. Run evaluation to confirm correctness and get the initial cost (area_delay_product).
4. ONLY after achieving 100% correctness, try to optimize the design to reduce the area_delay_product.
5. After each optimization, run evaluation to verify correctness is maintained and check the new cost.
6. If an optimization breaks correctness, revert and try a different approach.
7. Keep iterating until you run out of steps — use every step to try improvements. The run ends automatically at the step limit; your best passing design is kept.

In general, be creative and take risk. At least after you have a correct design, try bold optimizations to reduce area_delay_product, even if they might break correctness. You can always revert if it doesn't work.
The best results you obtain which is 100% correctness counts.
Try to beat at least the straightforward implementation.

Running evaluation is crucial after every change to ensure correctness and track cost. Do not skip evaluations.

You may do some 'thinking' alongside with your tool calling responses to explain your reasoning, but always remember to call a tool in every response.

## Important
- Always call a tool in every response.
- You have a maximum of **60 steps** (LLM turns). Budget them wisely: get a correct design first, then optimise. You may batch tool calls in one response --- at most 8, and only one `run_evaluation`.
- The best result is the one with lowest cost among designs that pass 100% of testbench checks.
- Use different filenames for different design variants so you can retrieve them later.
- If context files (e.g. component source files) are in your workspace, you can modify them directly using edit_file or replace_file to optimize the design. Make targeted, incremental changes rather than rewriting files from scratch — small edits are less likely to introduce bugs.
- Write clean, synthesizable RTL code.
- You can pass `target_delay` (ps) to `run_evaluation` to try different synthesis timing constraints; experimenting with this may improve PPA results (lower target delay may reduce delay, higher target delay may reduce area/transistor count). target_delay must be > 0.
- Do not spend too many steps sweeping `target_delay` — basically if you want to minimize delay choose a low value,if you want to minimize area/transistor count choose a high value. That's it.A few targeted experiments are more productive than an exhaustive sweep.

## Delay Optimization Tips
- `target_delay` trades area against delay (a lower value pressures toward faster logic, a higher value lets Yosys re-map to smaller cells). Do not overly iterate on different target delays; often the RTL structure is the real lever, target_delay is only a minor secondary knob.
- After getting a correct design, try different accumulation orderings (sequential vs. tree) and compare results — Yosys synthesis can produce noticeably different delays (~10 ps) from structurally similar descriptions.
- For designs dominated by a multiply-accumulate chain, the critical path is the multiplier followed by the adder tree. Reducing the adder depth or splitting into parallel partial sums can help.

## Reference Implementations
The following are reference implementations for inspiration. Study them for patterns and techniques, but do not copy them directly — adapt ideas to your specific design.

### Parallel-prefix adder (ripple-carry / carry-lookahead) (Add.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Binary adder using ripple-carry or parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.
// S = A+B

module Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);

	// Function: Binary adder using parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.

	logic [width-1:0] GI, PI;  // prefix gen./prop. in
	logic [width-1:0] GO, PO;  // prefix gen./prop. out
	logic [width-1:0] PT;  // adder propagate temp

	// Internal signals for unsigned operands
	logic [width-1:0] Auns, Buns, Suns;

	// default ripple-carry adder as slow implementation
	if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin
		// type conversion: std_logic_vector -> unsigned
		assign Auns = A;
		assign Buns = B;

		// addition
		assign Suns = Auns + Buns;

		// type conversion: unsigned -> std_logic_vector
		assign S = Suns;
	end else begin
		// parallel-prefix adders as medium and fast implementations

		// calculate prefix input generate/propagate signals
		assign GI = A & B;
		assign PI = A | B;
		// calculate adder propagate signals (PT = A xor B)
		assign PT = ~GI & PI;

		// calculate prefix output generate/propagate signals
		PrefixAndOr #(
			.width(width),
			.speed(speed)
		) prefix (
			.GI(GI),
			.PI(PI),
			.GO(GO),
			.PO(PO)
		);

		// calculate sum bits
		assign S = PT ^ {GO[width-2:0], 1'b0};
	end
endmodule



module behavioural_Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);
	assign S = A + B;
endmodule
```

### Prefix AND-OR structures (Sklansky, Brent-Kung, serial) (PrefixAndOr.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Prefix structures of different depth (i.e. speed) for carry calculation
// in binary adders. Compute in m levels new generate/propagate signal pairs
// for always larger groups of bits. Generate signals of the last level correspond
// to carries in binary addition. Basic logic operations: AND-OR for generate
// signals, AND for propagate signals.

module PrefixAndOr #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] GI,  // gen./prop. in
	input  logic [width-1:0] PI,  // gen./prop. in
	output logic [width-1:0] GO,  // gen./prop. out
	output logic [width-1:0] PO   // gen./prop. out
);

	// Constants
	localparam int n = width;  // prefix structure width
	localparam int m = $clog2(width);  // prefix structure depth

	// Sklansky parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::FAST) begin : fastPrefix
		logic [(m+1)*n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
			assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
			assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;
			for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels
				for (genvar k = 0; k < 2 ** (m - l); k++) begin : groups
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2 ** (l - 1); i++) begin : bits
						// pass prop and gen to following nodes
						if ((k * 2 ** l + i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = GT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
							assign PT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = PT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
						end
						// calculate new propagate and generate
						if ((k * 2 ** l + 2 ** (l - 1) + i) < n) begin : black
							assign GT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
												GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
											  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												 & GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1] );
							assign PT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
													PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1];
						end
					end
				end
			end
			assign GO = GT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
			assign PO = PT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
	end

	// Brent-Kung parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::MEDIUM) begin : mediumPrefix
		logic [(2*m)*n -1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
		assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;

		for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels1
			for (genvar k = 0; k < 2**(m-l); k++) begin : groups
				for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**l -1; i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**l +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
					end // white
				end // bits
				if ((k* 2**l + 2**l -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l - 1] |
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							     & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**(l-1) -1]);
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) -1];
				end // black
			end
		end // level1
		for (genvar l = m +1; l < 2*m; l++) begin : levels2
			for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
				if (i < n) begin : white
					assign GT[l*n +i] = GT[(l-1)*n +i];
					assign PT[l*n +i] = PT[(l-1)*n +i];
				end // white
			end // bits
			for (genvar k = 1; k < 2**(l-m); k++) begin : groups
				if (l < 2*m -1) begin : empty
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l -1) -1; i++) begin : bits
						if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
							assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						end // white
					end
				end // empty
				if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l -1) -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m-l-1) -1] 
								 & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) -1] );
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) -1];
				end // black
				for (genvar i = 2**(2*m -l-1); i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
					end // white
				end
			end
		end // level2
		assign GO = GT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
		assign PO = PT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
	end  // Serial-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	else if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin : slowPrefix
		logic [n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[0] = GI[0];
		assign PT[0] = PI[0];
		
		for (genvar i = 1; i < n; i++) begin : bits
			assign GT[i] = GI[i] | (PI[i] & GT[i-1]);
			assign PT[i] = PI[i] & PT[i-1];
		end
		assign GO = GT;
		assign PO = PT;
	end

endmodule

```

[Step 1/60]
//#############################################################################
// Copyright: Zero ASIC. All rights Reserved.
// Author: Andreas Olofsson
// License:  MIT (see LICENSE file in LogikBench repository)
//#############################################################################
//
// Processing element (PE) for the weight-stationary systolic array (TPU MXU).
//
// Each PE holds one stationary signed weight 'w'. Two independent datapaths
// share the cell:
//
//   Weight load (ld): the weights form a per-column shift register that loads
//   from the top. On each ld cycle the PE captures the weight from the PE above
//   (w_north) and presents its stored weight downward (w_south). After N load
//   cycles every PE in a column holds its final stationary weight.
//
//   Compute (en): the activation flows west to east (registered, one hop per
//   cycle) and the partial sum flows north to south, accumulating the local
//   product a_west * w. This is the only arithmetic cell in the array: one
//   signed DW x DW multiply plus an ACCW-bit add.
//
//#############################################################################

module tpu_pe
  #(parameter DW = 8,	 // operand width (signed)
    parameter ACCW = 32) // accumulator / partial-sum width (signed)
   (
    input		  clk,
    input		  rst,	      // synchronous, active high
    input		  ld,	      // weight-load shift enable
    input		  en,	      // compute enable
    input [DW-1:0]	  w_north,    // weight in from PE above
    input [DW-1:0]	  a_west,     // activation in from west
    input [ACCW-1:0]	  psum_north, // partial sum in from above
    output [DW-1:0]	  w_south,    // stored weight out to PE below
    output reg [DW-1:0]	  a_east,     // activation out to east
    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south  // partial sum out to below
    );

   // stationary weight register; presented downward for the load shift chain
   reg [DW-1:0] w;
   assign w_south = w;

   // local product: the ONLY signed operation. Operands are interpreted as
   // signed here via $signed; the 2*DW-bit result holds the two's-complement
   // product, which is then sign-extended to the accumulator width below.
   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);

   always @(posedge clk) begin
      if (rst) begin
         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};
         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};
         psum_south <= {ACCW{1'b0}};
      end
      else begin
         if (ld)
           w <= w_north;
         if (en) begin
            a_east     <= a_west;
            psum_south <= psum_north +
                          {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};
         end
      end
   end

endmodule
//#############################################################################
// Copyright: Zero ASIC. All rights Reserved.
// Author: Andreas Olofsson
// License:  MIT (see LICENSE file in LogikBench repository)
//#############################################################################
//
// N x N weight-stationary systolic array (the TPU MXU core).
//
// One tpu_pe per grid element, wired as a 2D mesh (per CLAUDE.md: replicated
// hardware via generate, never a procedural unroll):
//
//   - weights load from the top: w_top[j] feeds column j; each column is a
//     vertical shift register (w_south of PE(i,j) -> w_north of PE(i+1,j)).
//   - activations flow west to east: a_left[i] enters row i; a_east of PE(i,j)
//     feeds a_west of PE(i,j+1).
//   - partial sums flow north to south and accumulate; the top row's psum_north
//     is tied to zero (fresh accumulation), and c_bot[j] is the column-j result
//     leaving the bottom row.
//
// With weight B[i][j] held at PE(i,j) and activation A[m][i] entering row i,
// column j computes C[m][j] = sum_i A[m][i] * B[i][j]. Input/output skew (which
// time-aligns rows and columns) lives in the tpu top, not here.
//
//#############################################################################

module tpu_array
  #(parameter N = 8,	 // array dimension (N x N PEs)
    parameter DW = 8,	 // operand width (signed)
    parameter ACCW = 32) // accumulator width (signed)
   (
    input		clk,
    input		rst,	// synchronous, active high
    input		ld,	// weight-load shift enable
    input		en,	// compute enable
    input [N*DW-1:0]	w_top,	// weight inputs, one per column (load)
    input [N*DW-1:0]	a_left,	// activation inputs, one per row
    output [N*ACCW-1:0]	c_bot	// column results, one per column
    );

   // mesh nets: a_h carries activations east, psum_v carries sums south,
   // w_v carries weights south during load.
   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];
   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];
   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];

   genvar	   i, j;
   generate
      // west edge: activation inputs into column 0 of each row
      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west
         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];
      end
      // top edge: zero partial sums, weight inputs; bottom edge: results out

      // top edge: zero partial sums, weight inputs; bottom edge: results out
      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge

         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];
         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = psum_v[N][j];
      end

      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row
         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col
            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe

               .rst        (rst),

               .en         (en),

               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),
               .psum_south (psum_v[i+1][j]));
         end
      end
   endgenerate

endmodule
//#############################################################################
// Copyright: Zero ASIC. All rights Reserved.
// Author: Andreas Olofsson
// License:  MIT (see LICENSE file in LogikBench repository)
//#############################################################################
//
// Weight-stationary systolic matrix-multiply tile (a Google-TPU-style MXU).
//
// Computes one tile product C = A * B, where A and B are N x N signed operands
// (DW bits) and C is N x N signed (ACCW bits). B is the "weight" matrix, loaded
// into and held by the array; A is the "activation" matrix, streamed through; C
// drains out the bottom. Built from a tpu_array of N x N tpu_pe cells.
//
// Operation has two phases the host drives via valids:
//
//   1. Weight load: assert w_valid for N cycles. w_data carries one weight row
//      per cycle, N signed weights packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }. Because the
//      array loads top-down (shift south), push the BOTTOM matrix row first:
//      cycle k carries B[N-1-k][*]. After N cycles PE(i,j) holds B[i][j].
//
//   2. Compute: assert a_valid while streaming A one row per cycle, a_data
//      packed { col N-1 ... col 0 } = A[m][N-1..0]. Row m of the result, C[m][*]
//      (packed the same way), appears on c_data with c_valid high, 2*N-1 cycles
//      after its A row was accepted.
//
// Time alignment: row i of A is delayed i cycles entering the array (input
// skew) so partial sums chain correctly down each column; column j of the
// result is delayed N-1-j cycles (output de-skew) so a full result row leaves
// together. Both skew networks and the valid pipeline advance only with the
// array (compute enable), so the 2*N-1 latency is exact. Per CLAUDE.md the skew
// chains are generated per-row/column shift registers, not a procedural unroll.
//
//#############################################################################

module tpu
  #(parameter N = 8,	 // array dimension (N x N)
    parameter DW = 8,	 // operand width (signed)
    parameter ACCW = 32) // result / accumulator width (signed)
   (
    input		clk,
    input		rst,	 // synchronous, active high
    input		w_valid, // weight-row valid (load phase)
    input [N*DW-1:0]	w_data,	 // weight row, packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }
    input		a_valid, // activation-row valid (compute phase)
    input [N*DW-1:0]	a_data,	 // activation row, packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }
    output		c_valid, // result-row valid
    output [N*ACCW-1:0]	c_data	 // result row, packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }
    );

   // total injection-to-output latency, in compute cycles
   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;

   // load weights while w_valid; otherwise the array computes (and drains)
   wire	      ld = w_valid;
   wire	      en = ~w_valid;

   //##########################################################################
   // Input skew: row i of the activation is delayed i cycles. Zero is injected
   // when a_valid is low so idle/drain cycles add nothing to the accumulators.
   //##########################################################################
   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;
   genvar	   i;
   generate
      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew
         wire [DW-1:0] din =
		       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};
         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0
            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;
         end
         else begin : g_skewn
            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];
            integer k;
            always @(posedge clk) begin
               if (rst) begin
                  for (k = 0; k < i; k = k + 1)
                    sr[k] <= {DW{1'b0}};
               end
               else if (en) begin
                  sr[0] <= din;
                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)
                    sr[k] <= sr[k-1];
               end
            end
            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = sr[i-1];
         end
      end
   endgenerate

   //##########################################################################
   // The systolic array.
   //##########################################################################
   wire [N*ACCW-1:0] c_bot;
   tpu_array #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_array
     (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .ld(ld), .en(en),
      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), .c_bot(c_bot));

   //##########################################################################
   // Output de-skew: column j is delayed N-1-j cycles so all columns of a
   // result row line up. Column N-1 (longest path) needs no delay.
   //##########################################################################
   genvar j;
   generate
      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_deskew
         localparam D = N - 1 - j;
         wire [ACCW-1:0] cin = c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW];
         if (D == 0) begin : g_deskew0
            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = cin;
         end
         else begin : g_deskewn
            reg [ACCW-1:0] dr [0:D-1];
            integer k;
            always @(posedge clk) begin
               if (rst) begin
                  for (k = 0; k < D; k = k + 1)
                    dr[k] <= {ACCW{1'b0}};
               end
               else if (en) begin
                  dr[0] <= cin;
                  for (k = 1; k < D; k = k + 1)
                    dr[k] <= dr[k-1];
               end
            end
            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = dr[D-1];
         end
      end
   endgenerate

   //##########################################################################
   // Valid pipeline: c_valid is a_valid delayed LAT compute cycles.
   //##########################################################################
   reg [LAT-1:0] vpipe;
   always @(posedge clk) begin
      if (rst)
        vpipe <= {LAT{1'b0}};
      else if (en)
        vpipe <= {vpipe[LAT-2:0], a_valid};
   end
   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];

endmodule

[Step 3/60]
// Self-checking testbench for the tpu benchmark (8x8 weight-stationary
// systolic matmul tile). Reads vectors.dat, one line per clock cycle:
//   w_valid w_data(64b) a_valid a_data(64b)   c_valid c_data(256b)   (hex)
// The expected outputs are the ORIGINAL logikbench tpu's per-cycle behavior;
// c_valid and c_data are compared CYCLE-ACCURATELY every cycle (c_data only
// while c_valid is high — the original holds stale data between results).
module tb;
  localparam N = 8, DW = 8, ACCW = 32;
  int total_checks;
  int total_errors;

  logic clk, rst;
  logic w_valid, a_valid, c_valid;
  logic [N*DW-1:0] w_data, a_data;
  logic [N*ACCW-1:0] c_data;

  tpu dut
    (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .w_valid(w_valid), .w_data(w_data),
     .a_valid(a_valid), .a_data(a_data), .c_valid(c_valid), .c_data(c_data));

  initial clk = 0;
  always #5 clk = ~clk;

  integer fd, rc, line_num;
  string line_buf;
  logic s_wv, s_av, e_cv;
  logic [63:0] s_wd, s_ad;
  logic [255:0] e_cd;

  initial begin
    total_checks = 0;
    total_errors = 0;
    w_valid = 0; a_valid = 0; w_data = 0; a_data = 0;
    rst = 1;
    repeat (4) @(negedge clk);
    rst = 0;

    fd = $fopen("vectors.dat", "r");
    if (fd == 0) begin
      $display("ERROR: cannot open vectors.dat");
      $fatal(1);
    end
    line_num = 0;
    while (!$feof(fd)) begin
      line_num = line_num + 1;
      void'($fgets(line_buf, fd));
      if (line_buf.len() == 0) continue;
      if (line_buf.substr(0, 0) == "#") continue;
      rc = $sscanf(line_buf, "%h %h %h %h %h %h", s_wv, s_wd, s_av, s_ad, e_cv, e_cd);
      if (rc != 6) continue;
      w_valid = s_wv; w_data = s_wd; a_valid = s_av; a_data = s_ad;
      @(negedge clk);
      total_checks = total_checks + 1;
      if (c_valid !== e_cv || (e_cv && c_data !== e_cd)) begin
        $display("TB_ERROR line=%0d exp(cv=%b cd=%h) got(cv=%b cd=%h)",
                 line_num, e_cv, e_cd, c_valid, c_data);
        total_errors = total_errors + 1;
        if (total_errors > 20) begin
          $display("TB_SUMMARY total=%0d errors=%0d", total_checks, total_errors);
          $fatal(1, "FAIL (too many errors, aborting)");
        end
      end
    end
    $fclose(fd);

    $display("TB_SUMMARY total=%0d errors=%0d", total_checks, total_errors);
    if (total_errors != 0) $fatal(1, "FAIL");
    $display("PASS");
    $finish;
  end
endmodule

[ASSISTANT]
Now let me try a key optimization: narrowing the internal accumulator width. The maximum partial sum (8 products of ±16384) is ±131072, which fits in 19-bit signed. Using 20-bit internal accumulators instead of 32-bit should reduce both area (smaller registers/adders) and delay (shorter carry chain).
  -> create_file(content="// TPU with narrow internal accumulator (20-bit instead of 32-bit)\n// Max partial sum = 8 * 128*128 = 131072 = 2^17, fits in 19-bit signed.\n// Using 20-bit for safety margin.\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 20)  // narrow internal accumulator\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_south <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east     <= a_west;\n            psum_south <= psum_north +\n                          {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 20)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = psum_v[N][j];\n      end\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .rst        (rst),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_north (psum_v[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_south (psum_v[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)   // output width (fixed by spec)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   localparam ACCW_INT = 20;  // narrow internal accumulator\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   // Input skew\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew\n         wire [DW-1:0] din =\n\t\t       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;\n         end\n         else begin : g_skewn\n            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= {DW{1'b0}};\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  sr[0] <= din;\n                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= sr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = sr[i-1];\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   // Systolic array with narrow accumulator\n   wire [N*ACCW_INT-1:0] c_bot;\n   tpu_array #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW_INT)) u_array\n     (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .ld(ld), .en(en),\n      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), .c_bot(c_bot));\n\n   // Output de-skew with narrow registers, sign-extend to full width at output\n   genvar j;\n   generate\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_deskew\n         localparam D = N - 1 - j;\n         wire [ACCW_INT-1:0] cin = c_bot[j*ACCW_INT +: ACCW_INT];\n         if (D == 0) begin : g_deskew0\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] =\n               {{(ACCW-ACCW_INT){cin[ACCW_INT-1]}}, cin};\n         end\n         else begin : g_deskewn\n            reg [ACCW_INT-1:0] dr [0:D-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; k < D; k = k + 1)\n                    dr[k] <= {ACCW_INT{1'b0}};\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  dr[0] <= cin;\n                  for (k = 1; k < D; k = k + 1)\n                    dr[k] <= dr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            wire [ACCW_INT-1:0] dout = dr[D-1];\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] =\n               {{(ACCW-ACCW_INT){dout[ACCW_INT-1]}}, dout};\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   // Valid pipeline\n   reg [LAT-1:0] vpipe;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst)\n        vpipe <= {LAT{1'b0}};\n      else if (en)\n        vpipe <= {vpipe[LAT-2:0], a_valid};\n   end\n   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];\n\nendmodule\n", filename='design_narrow.v')

  -> create_file(content="// TPU with 18-bit internal accumulator\n// Max partial sum = 8 * 128*128 = 131072 = 2^17, fits in 18-bit signed (range -2^17 to 2^17-1)\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 18)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_south <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east     <= a_west;\n            psum_south <= psum_north +\n                          {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 18)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; [ASSISTANT]
The default target_delay=500 is optimal. Now let me try a major optimization: removing unnecessary resets from internal registers. Only the valid pipeline (vpipe) needs reset to ensure c_valid=0 at startup. All other registers (weights, activations, psums, skew, deskew) are overwritten with correct values before they affect any checked output.
  -> create_file(content="// TPU with 19-bit internal accumulator and minimal resets\n// Only vpipe (valid pipeline) is reset; all other registers are don't-reset.\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (ld)\n        w <= w_north;\n      if (en) begin\n         a_east     <= a_west;\n         psum_south <= psum_north +\n                       {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = psum_v[N][j];\n      end\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .rst        (rst),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_north (psum_v[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_south (psum_v[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   localparam ACCW_INT = 19;\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   // Input skew - no reset needed (zeroed when a_valid=0)\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew\n         wire [DW-1:0] din =\n\t\t       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;\n         end\n         else begin : g_skewn\n            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (en) begin\n                  sr[0] <= din;\n                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= sr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = sr[i-1];\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   // Systolic array with narrow accumulator\n   wire [N*ACCW_INT-1:0] c_bot;\n   tpu_array #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW_INT)) u_array\n     (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .ld(ld), .en(en),\n      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), .c_bot(c_bot));\n\n   // Output de-skew - no reset needed (overwritten before c_valid goes high)\n   genvar j;\n   generate\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_deskew\n         localparam D = N - 1 - j;\n         wire [ACCW_INT-1:0] cin = c_bot[j*ACCW_INT +: ACCW_INT];\n         if (D == 0) begin : g_deskew0\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] =\n               {{(ACCW-ACCW_INT){cin[ACCW_INT-1]}}, cin};\n         end\n         else begin : g_deskewn\n            reg [ACCW_INT-1:0] dr [0:D-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (en) begin\n                  dr[0] <= cin;\n                  for (k = 1; k < D; k = k + 1)\n                    dr[k] <= dr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            wire [ACCW_INT-1:0] dout = dr[D-1];\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] =\n               {{(ACCW-ACCW_INT){dout[ACCW_INT-1]}}, dout};\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   // Valid pipeline - MUST be reset to ensure c_valid=0 at startup\n   reg [LAT-1:0] vpipe;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst)\n        vpipe <= {LAT{1'b0}};\n      else if (en)\n        vpipe <= {vpipe[LAT-2:0], a_valid};\n   end\n   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];\n\nendmodule\n", filename='design_noreset.v')

  -> create_file(content="// TPU with 19-bit accumulator, no resets except vpipe, flat skew/deskew regs\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (ld)\n        w <= w_north;\n      if (en) begin\n         a_east     <= a_west;\n         psum_south <= psum_north +\n                       {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = psum_v[N][j];\n      end\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_north (psum_v[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_south (psum_v[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   localparam ACCW_INT = 19;\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   // Input skew using flat packed shift registers per row\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew\n         wire [DW-1:0] din =\n\t\t       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;\n         end\n         else begin : g_skewn\n            // i stages of delay: pack into i*DW bits\n            reg [i*DW-1:0] sr;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (en)\n                 sr <= {sr[(i-1)*DW-1:0], din};\n            end\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = sr[(i-1)*DW +: DW];\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   // Systolic array with narrow accumulator\n   wire [N*ACCW_INT-1:0] c_bot;\n   tpu_array #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW_INT)) u_array\n     (.clk(clk), .ld(ld), .en(en),\n      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), .c_bot(c_bot));\n\n   // Output de-skew using flat packed shift registers per column\n   genvar j;\n   generate\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_deskew\n         localparam D = N - 1 - j;\n         wire [ACCW_INT-1:0] cin = c_bot[j*ACCW_INT +: ACCW_INT];\n         if (D == 0) begin : g_deskew0\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] =\n               {{(ACCW-ACCW_INT){cin[ACCW_INT-1]}}, cin};\n         end\n         else begin : g_deskewn\n            reg [D*ACCW_INT-1:0] dr;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (en)\n                 dr <= {dr[(D-1)*ACCW_INT-1:0], cin};\n            end\n            wire [ACCW_INT-1:0] dout = dr[(D-1)*ACCW_INT +: ACCW_INT];\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] =\n               {{(ACCW-ACCW_INT){dout[ACCW_INT-1]}}, dout};\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   // Valid pipeline - MUST be reset\n   reg [LAT-1:0] vpipe;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst)\n        vpipe <= {LAT{1'b0}};\n      else if (en)\n        vpipe <= {vpipe[LAT-2:0], a_valid};\n   end\n   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];\n\nendmodule\n", filename='design_flat.v')

[ASSISTANT]
Flat registers didn't help much (3.22M vs 3.18M). The best is still design_noreset at 3.18M. Let me try a radical optimization: since the critical path goes through the weight register → multiplier → adder, I can try registering the multiplication separately (pipelining the PE). But that would change cycle-accurate behavior.

Instead, let me try reducing the partial sum width further. The key insight: the psum going into the PE is from above, so at PE row 0 the psum is 0, at row 1 it's one product (16-bit), at row 2 it's two products, etc. The maximum partial sum at row k is k products of ±16384 = k * 16384. At the bottom (row 7), max is 7 * 16384 = 114688, and at row 7's output (after adding) it's 8 * 16384 = 131072. But row 7's output goes to c_bot (final result). The intermediate psums at row k only need ceil(log2(k*16384)) + 1 = ceil(log2(k)) + 15 bits.

Actually, the standard way to optimize this is to use 19-bit accumulators throughout, which I already do. Let me think about the critical path more carefully.

The critical path is: weight_q → multiply → sign-extend → add → register. The multiply of 8x8 signed is the biggest contributor. Let me try using an explicit Booth-encoded multiplier or a different multiply structure.

Actually, let me try a simple but effective approach: use a registered product. Instead of computing `prod` and `psum_north + prod` in the same cycle, I can split: register `prod` first, then add in the next cycle. But this would change the pipeline depth...

Actually, a better idea: in a weight-stationary array, the weight `w` is held in a register. The activation `a_west` comes from the neighboring PE (also registered). So the multiply inputs are both registered. The product goes into a combinational add with psum_north. The psum chain depth is 8 (one add per PE in a column). 

Let me try a different multiplier decomposition - use a Baugh-Wooley or modified Booth multiplier that might synthesize to faster logic:
  -> create_file(content="// TPU with explicit split of product computation to help synthesis\n// Product = a * w (signed 8x8 = 16-bit)\n// We compute the product using AND/XOR array logic explicitly\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   // Signed multiply via sign extension trick:\n   // For signed multiply, sign-extend both operands to DW+1 bits, multiply, take lower 2*DW bits\n   // This avoids the sign correction logic in a naive multiplier\n   wire signed [DW:0] a_ext = $signed(a_west);\n   wire signed [DW:0] w_ext = $signed(w);\n   wire [2*DW+1:0] prod_full = a_ext * w_ext;\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = prod_full[2*DW-1:0];\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (ld)\n        w <= w_north;\n      if (en) begin\n         a_east     <= a_west;\n         psum_south <= psum_north +\n                       {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = psum_v[N][j];\n      end\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_north (psum_v[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_south (psum_v[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   localparam ACCW_INT = 19;\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew\n         wire [DW-1:0] din =\n\t\t       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;\n         end\n         else begin : g_skewn\n            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (en) begin\n                  sr[0] <= din;\n                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= sr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = sr[i-1];\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   wire [N*ACCW_INT-1:0] c_bot;\n   tpu_array #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW_INT)) u_array\n     (.clk(clk), .ld(ld), .en(en),\n      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), .c_bot(c_bot));\n\n   genvar j;\n   generate\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_deskew\n         localparam D = N - 1 - j;\n         wire [ACCW_INT-1:0] cin = c_bot[j*ACCW_INT +: ACCW_INT];\n         if (D == 0) begin : g_deskew0\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] =\n               {{(ACCW-ACCW_INT){cin[ACCW_INT-1]}}, cin};\n         end\n         else begin : g_deskewn\n            reg [ACCW_INT-1:0] dr [0:D-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (en) begin\n                  dr[0] <= cin;\n                  for (k = 1; k < D; k = k + 1)\n                    dr[k] <= dr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            wire [ACCW_INT-1:0] dout = dr[D-1];\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] =\n               {{(ACCW-ACCW_INT){dout[ACCW_INT-1]}}, dout};\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   reg [LAT-1:0] vpipe;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst)\n        vpipe <= {LAT{1'b0}};\n      else if (en)\n        vpipe <= {vpipe[LAT-2:0], a_valid};\n   end\n   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];\n\nendmodule\n", filename='design_signext.v')

[ASSISTANT]
3.20M - worse. Let me go back to the best design (design_noreset) and try a carry-save accumulator approach to break the carry chain. In a CSA, the partial sum stays in redundant form (sum + carry), and only the final addition happens at the output:
  -> create_file(content="// TPU with carry-save accumulator chain to break the carry chain\n// Each PE maintains psum in redundant (sum, carry) form\n// Final propagate-add at the bottom of each column\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north_s,  // sum part from above\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north_c,  // carry part from above\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south_s, // sum part to below\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south_c  // carry part to below\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   // Product (16-bit signed)\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n\n   // CSA: sum_out = psum_north_s ^ psum_north_c ^ prod_ext\n   //      carry_out = (psum_north_s & psum_north_c) | (psum_north_s & prod_ext) | (psum_north_c & prod_ext)\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] cs_s = psum_north_s ^ psum_north_c ^ prod_ext;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] cs_c = (psum_north_s & psum_north_c) |\n                          (psum_north_s & prod_ext) |\n                          (psum_north_c & prod_ext);\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (ld)\n        w <= w_north;\n      if (en) begin\n         a_east     <= a_west;\n         psum_south_s <= cs_s;\n         psum_south_c <= {cs_c[ACCW-2:0], 1'b0}; // carry shifted left\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_s [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_c [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_north_s (psum_s[i][j]),\n               .psum_north_c (psum_c[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_south_s (psum_s[i+1][j]),\n               .psum_south_c (psum_c[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   localparam ACCW_INT = 19;\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; [ASSISTANT]
Default 500 is still the best. Let me try the CSA approach but with a wider accumulator to handle the carry-save correctly. Actually, let me check if the carry-save might need a wider width to prevent overflow. The CSA uses 19-bit sum and 19-bit carry, but the carry is shifted left, so the carry can propagate beyond 19 bits.

The actual max value is 131072 = 2^17. In 19-bit signed representation, we can represent up to 2^18-1 = 262143, so the sum of sum+carry must fit in 19-bit signed. With 8 CSAs, the maximum redundant value is... let me think. Each CSA adds a product (±16384). After 8 additions, max is ±131072. In redundant form, the sum part can be up to 131072 and carry part similar, so the total could be up to 2*131072 = 262144 which is just above 2^18-1=262143. But 262144 = 2^18 which can't be represented in 19-bit signed. 

However, the final add is `psum_s + psum_c` which would produce 20-bit result. The issue is the 19-bit registers might overflow. Let me check - the carry is shifted left by 1, so carry[N-1] is always 0 (since we shift in 0). This means the effective range is correct. Let me verify it actually passes (it does pass all tests, so 19-bit is enough for the test vectors).

Let me now try to reduce the area of the CSA design. The carry chain is broken, but the final ripple adder adds delay. Let me try making the final adder a fast (prefix) adder. Or better, let me try the CSA with 18-bit width to see if it still works:
  -> create_file(content="// TPU with CSA accumulator, 18-bit internal width\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 18)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north_s,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north_c,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south_s,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south_c\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] cs_s = psum_north_s ^ psum_north_c ^ prod_ext;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] cs_c = (psum_north_s & psum_north_c) |\n                          (psum_north_s & prod_ext) |\n                          (psum_north_c & prod_ext);\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (ld)\n        w <= w_north;\n      if (en) begin\n         a_east     <= a_west;\n         psum_south_s <= cs_s;\n         psum_south_c <= {cs_c[ACCW-2:0], 1'b0};\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 18)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_s [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_c [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_s[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign psum_c[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = psum_s[N][j] + psum_c[N][j];\n      end\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_north_s (psum_s[i][j]),\n               .psum_north_c (psum_c[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_south_s (psum_s[i+1][j]),\n               .psum_south_c (psum_c[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   localparam ACCW_INT = 18;\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew\n         wire [DW-1:0] din =\n\t\t       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;\n         end\n         else begin : g_skewn\n            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (en) begin\n                  sr[0] <= din;\n                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= sr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = sr[i-1];\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   wire [N*ACCW_INT-1:0] c_bot;\n   tpu_array #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW_INT)) u_array\n     (.clk(clk), .ld(ld), .en(en),\n      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), .c_bot(c_bot));\n\n   genvar j;\n   generate\n      for (j = 0; 18-bit overflows, 19-bit is the minimum. Let me go back to the CSA design and try optimizations to reduce area while keeping the delay benefit. The area is 4306 vs 3869 for the ripple version. The CSA doubles the psum registers (2x 19-bit per PE = 64 extra PEs × 19 bits = 1216 bits of extra registers). Let me try using a hybrid: CSA for the middle PEs and ripple at the ends. Actually, let me try something different - move the final ripple adder into the deskew stage to share it.

Wait, a better idea: let me use a narrower CSA width. The key observation is that the carry-save representation doesn't need to represent the full signed range - the sum and carry parts are unsigned-like. The maximum value of sum+carry after 8 additions is 131072. In 19-bit signed, 131072 is 0_0010_0000_0000_0000 = 2^17. The 19-bit representation handles this fine since 19-bit signed max is 262143 = 2^18-1.

Let me try a different approach: reduce the multiplier width. The product of two 8-bit signed values is 16 bits. But we only need the lower 16 bits sign-extended. Since the accumulator only needs 19 bits, and the product is 16 bits, we sign-extend to 19. That's already what we do.

Let me try another optimization: the critical path in the CSA design goes through the final ripple adder at the bottom. Let me try using a faster adder there, or even better, move the final addition to the deskew chain (it's already registered there).

Actually, let me try a radically different approach: use the original ripple design but with an 8-bit carry-save only for the multiplier partial products, combining the carry into the psum chain. Or even simpler - let me try using `prod + psum_north` directly but with a specific adder structure.

Let me instead try to optimize the CSA design by reducing the product width to only what's needed:
  -> create_file(content="// TPU with CSA accumulator, 19-bit, product width optimization\n// Product is 16-bit signed, we only need to add it to 19-bit psum\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north_s,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north_c,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south_s,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south_c\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   // Compute product using unsigned multiply + sign correction\n   // prod = (a * w_unsigned) adjusted for sign\n   // For signed: if a negative, subtract a*|w|; if w negative, subtract |a|*w\n   // Actually just use the signed multiply, it's simpler\n   wire signed [DW-1:0] a_s = $signed(a_west);\n   wire signed [DW-1:0] w_s = $signed(w);\n   \n   // Full adder CSA: 3:2 compress\n   // Only the lower 16 bits of product matter (bits [15:0])\n   // sign-extend to ACCW\n   wire [15:0] prod16 = a_s * w_s;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(ACCW-16){prod16[15]}}, prod16};\n\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] cs_s = psum_north_s ^ psum_north_c ^ prod_ext;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] cs_c = (psum_north_s & psum_north_c) |\n                          (psum_north_s & prod_ext) |\n                          (psum_north_c & prod_ext);\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (ld)\n        w <= w_north;\n      if (en) begin\n         a_east     <= a_west;\n         psum_south_s <= cs_s;\n         psum_south_c <= {cs_c[ACCW-2:0], 1'b0};\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_s [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_c [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_s[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign psum_c[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = psum_s[N][j] + psum_c[N][j];\n      end\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_north_s (psum_s[i][j]),\n               .psum_north_c (psum_c[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_south_s (psum_s[i+1][j]),\n               .psum_south_c (psum_c[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   localparam ACCW_INT = 19;\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew\n         wire [DW-1:0] din =\n\t\t       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;\n         end\n         else begin : g_skewn\n            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (en) begin\n                  sr[0] <= din;\n                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= sr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = sr[i-1];\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   wire [N*ACCW_INT-1:0] c_bot;\n   tpu_array #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW_INT)) u_array\n     (.clk(clk), .ld(ld), .en(en),\n      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), .c_bot(c_bot));\n\n   genvar j;\n   generate\n      for (j = 0; The default 500 is still the best for CSA. Let me try to reduce the area of the CSA by using a narrower carry word. Since the carry is shifted left, the carry register doesn't need the MSB. Also, the product is 16-bit, so only bits [15:0] of the psum interact with the product directly; higher bits only carry. Actually, let me try a completely different approach. The critical path in the CSA design goes through: w_register → multiplier → CSA → CSA register → final_add → deskew_register. The multiplier is the biggest delay contributor. What if I pre-compute the product and register it, then the CSA happens the next cycle? This would add 1 cycle latency but I'd need to adjust the skew/deskew. Let me try:
  -> create_file(content="// TPU with registered product + CSA to break critical path\n// Product is computed and registered, then CSA happens next cycle\n// This adds 1 cycle to psum latency, compensated by 1 less deskew cycle\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north_s,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north_c,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south_s,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south_c\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n\n   // CSA\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] cs_s = psum_north_s ^ psum_north_c ^ prod_ext;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] cs_c = (psum_north_s & psum_north_c) |\n                          (psum_north_s & prod_ext) |\n                          (psum_north_c & prod_ext);\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (ld)\n        w <= w_north;\n      if (en) begin\n         a_east     <= a_west;\n         psum_south_s <= cs_s;\n         psum_south_c <= {cs_c[ACCW-2:0], 1'b0};\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_s [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_c [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_s[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign psum_c[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = psum_s[N][j] + psum_c[N][j];\n      end\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_north_s (psum_s[i][j]),\n               .psum_north_c (psum_c[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_south_s (psum_s[i+1][j]),\n               .psum_south_c (psum_c[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   localparam ACCW_INT = 19;\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew\n         wire [DW-1:0] din =\n\t\t       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;\n         end\n         else begin : g_skewn\n            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (en) begin\n                  sr[0] <= din;\n                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= sr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = sr[i-1];\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   wire [N*ACCW_INT-1:0] c_bot;\n   tpu_array #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW_INT)) u_array\n     (.clk(clk), .ld(ld), .en(en),\n      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), .c_bot(c_bot));\n\n   genvar j;\n   generate\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_deskew\n         localparam D = N - 1 - j;\n         wire [ACCW_INT-1:0] cin = c_bot[j*ACCW_INT +: ACCW_INT];\n         if (D == 0) begin : g_deskew0\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] =\n               {{(ACCW-ACCW_INT){cin[ACCW_INT-1]}}, cin};\n         end\n         else begin : g_deskewn\n            reg [ACCW_INT-1:0] dr [0:D-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (en) begin\n                  dr[0] <= cin;\n                  for (k = 1; k < D; k = k + 1)\n                    dr[k] <= dr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            wire [ACCW_INT-1:0] dout = dr[D-1];\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] =\n               {{(ACCW-ACCW_INT){dout[ACCW_INT-1]}}, dout};\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   reg [LAT-1:0] vpipe;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst)\n        vpipe <= {LAT{1'b0}};\n      else if (en)\n        vpipe <= {vpipe[LAT-2:0], a_valid};\n   end\n   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];\n\nendmodule\n", filename='design_csa_opt.v')

Also, let me try using a carry-save where the carry register only has 17 bits (since carry bit 0 is always 0, and the uppermost bit is always sign):
  -> create_file(content="// TPU with CSA split + narrow carry (17-bit: bits [17:1])\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19,\n    parameter PROD_W = 16,\n    parameter CARRY_W = 17)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north_s,\n    input [CARRY_W-1:0]  psum_north_c,  // bits [17:1]\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south_s,\n    output reg [CARRY_W-1:0] psum_south_c\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   wire prod_sign = prod[PROD_W-1];\n   \n   // Full carry reconstruction: {carry, 1'b0} with sign extension for bits [18:18]\n   // Actually, carry is bits [17:1] and bit 0 = 0, bit 18 = sign of carry\n   // But carry bit 18 can be non-zero from CSA. Let me just keep it simple:\n   // carry_full = {{1{psum_north_c[CARRY_W-1]}}, psum_north_c, 1'b0}\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_c_full = {{(ACCW-CARRY_W-1){psum_north_c[CARRY_W-1]}}, psum_north_c, 1'b0};\n   \n   wire [ACCW-1:0] cs_s = psum_north_s ^ psum_c_full ^ {{(ACCW-PROD_W){prod_sign}}, prod};\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] cs_c = (psum_north_s & psum_c_full) |\n                          (psum_north_s & {{(ACCW-PROD_W){prod_sign}}, prod}) |\n                          (psum_c_full & {{(ACCW-PROD_W){prod_sign}}, prod});\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (ld)\n        w <= w_north;\n      if (en) begin\n         a_east     <= a_west;\n         psum_south_s <= cs_s;\n         psum_south_c <= cs_c[ACCW-2:1]; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW), .CARRY_W(CARRY_W)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_north_s (psum_s[i][j]),\n               .psum_north_c (psum_c[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_south_s (psum_s[i+1][j]),\n               .psum_south_c (psum_c[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   localparam ACCW_INT = 19;\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; [ASSISTANT]
Default 500 is still best. Let me try another approach: reduce the sum register width too. Since we use 19-bit sum, but the max result is 2^17, the upper 2 bits (17, 18) are sign extension. Let me try 17-bit sum registers. Actually, that won't work because CSA needs to represent the carry-save redundancy which can be larger.

Let me try a different approach: combine the CSA split with narrower carry bits. The key insight is that in the split design, the carry register has 19 bits. The carry bit 0 is always 0 (shifted in), so we could save 1 bit. And bit 18 is the sign, which could be handled differently. Let me try keeping only bits [17:1] as carry, sign-extending bit 17 to bit 18:
